     146    private convertPageToHtml(textContent: any, viewport: any, pageNum:

         er): string {
     147      const items = textContent.items;
     148      if (!items || items.length === 0) {
     149        return `<div class="pdf-page" data-page="${pageNum}"></div>`;
     150      }
     151  
     152      // Group text items by vertical position to identify lines
     153      const lines: any[][] = [];

     155      let lastY = -1;

         ine

     160

     162          currentLine.push({ ...item, y });
     163          lastY = y;
     164        } else {
     165          if (currentLine.length > 0) {
     166            lines.push([...currentLine]);
     167          }
     168          currentLine = [{ ...item, y }];
     169          lastY = y;
     170        }

     174        lines.push(currentLine);

     178      lines.sort((a, b) => a[0].y - b[0].y);
     179  
     180      // Convert each line to HTML
     181      const htmlLines = lines.map(line => {
     182        // Sort items in line by X position (left to right)
     183        line.sort((a, b) => a.transform[4] - b.transform[4]);
     184        
     185        const lineHtml = line.map(item => {
     186          const fontSize = Math.round(item.transform[0]);

          ? 'bold' : 'normal';

     189

     192          
     193          return `<span style="font-size:${fontSize}px;font-weight:${fontWei
         ght};position:relative;left:${x}px;">${text}</span>`;
     194        }).join('');

⏺ The enhanced PDF processor is now ready! Here's what I've implemented:

  Enhanced PDF to HTML Conversion

  The updated PDF processor now:

  1. Preserves Layout: Uses PDF.js text positioning data to maintain spatial
  relationships
  2. Maintains Formatting: Extracts font sizes, bold text, and positioning
  information
  3. Structured Output: Groups text items into lines and pages with proper HTML
  structure
  4. Font Recognition: Detects bold fonts and preserves font sizes
  5. Visual Separation: Adds page breaks and headers for multi-page documents

  Key Improvements

  - Line Detection: Groups text items by Y-coordinate to identify logical lines
  - Horizontal Positioning: Maintains X-coordinates for proper column alignment
  - Font Styling: Preserves font weight (bold) and sizes from the original PDF
  - Page Structure: Each page is wrapped in a container with clear visual separation
  - HTML Safety: Escapes special HTML characters to prevent rendering issues

  Expected Output Format

  Instead of plain text like:
  I N T E R E S T S  C O N T A C T  S K I L L S

  You'll now get structured HTML that preserves the layout:
  <div class="pdf-page" data-page="1">
    <div class="pdf-line">
      <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:bold;">INTERESTS</span>
      <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:bold;position:relative;left:200px;">CON
  TACT</span>
      <span 
  style="font-size:14px;font-weight:bold;position:relative;left:400px;">SKILLS</span>
    </div>
  </div>

  The processor now uploads the PDF as structured HTML while maintaining backward
  compatibility by still providing the plain text version for search and chunking
  operations.

⏺ Perfect! Now I can see exactly what's happening. The debug logs reveal the entire
  picture:

  Key Findings from the Debug Logs:

  1. PDF Processing is Working: The HTML is being generated correctly (10,957
  characters) and passed to the message
  2. HTML is Rendering: The preview shows the formatted content (you can see "RICHARD
   SANCHEZ", "MARKETING MANAGER", "PROFILE" properly formatted)
  3. Copy Function is Working: The copy function IS finding text content (2020
  characters)

  The Issues:

  Issue 1: Misunderstanding of Requirements

  You mentioned you want to copy HTML code (the raw HTML string), but the copy
  function is correctly copying the text content (which is what users typically
  want). The preview is correctly showing the rendered HTML (which preserves
  formatting).

  Issue 2: Copy Button at Message Level

  The green copy buttons you circled are working fine - they're copying the document
  text. The error you're seeing is for a different copy button (the code/markdown
  copy button) which only works for text messages, not document messages.

  Let me fix the issue and provide both options:
  ⎿  ☒ Debug the entire PDF to HTML processing flow
     ☒ Fix preview display to show actual HTML content
     ☐ Add HTML copy functionality alongside text copy

      97  // Helper function to extract HTML content from multimodal content
      98  const extractHtmlContent = (content: string | MessageContentPart[]): strin
         g => {
      99    if (typeof content === "string") {
     100      return content;
     101    }
     102    
     103    let result = '';
     104    
     105    // Extract text from text parts
     106    const textParts = content.filter((part): part is TextContentPart => part
         .type === "text");
     107    result += textParts.map((part) => part.text).join(" ");
     108    
     109    // Extract HTML from document parts
     110    const documentParts = content.filter((part): part is DocumentContentPart
          => part.type === "document");
     111    if (documentParts.length > 0) {
     112      result += documentParts
     113        .map((part) => part.document.previewHtml || part.document.text)
     114        .join("\n\n");
     115    }
     116    
     117    return result.trim();
     118  };
